Michael B. Jordan's Fantasy Series On Prime Video Reinvents The Genre, And Some Fans Won't Like It

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

While Michael B. Jordan has a massive Prime Video fantasy show on the way soon, fans of the genre should be aware that the show will divide audiences as it relies heavily on a majorly controversial trope. Ever since HBO spent an unprecedented amount of money on the budget of Game of Thrones season 1 back in 2011, every streaming service and cable network has been working overtime to find the next truly gigantic fantasy series.

From Prime Video’s Lord of the Rings prequel The Rings of Power to Netflix’s The Witcher, creators spread no expense replicating and even outdoing the ambition of HBO’s A Song of Ice & Fire adaptation. However, while Prime Video’s Critical Role shows The Legend of Vox Machina and The Mighty Nein share the mature content and fantasy adventure appeal of early Game of Thrones, no single show has supplanted the HBO hit as the biggest fantasy series ever.

Even HBO’s own Harry Potter reboot may struggle to replicate the popularity of Game of Thrones, since the series is a retread of a highly familiar intellectual property rather than an exciting new fantasy project. In contrast, Prime Video’s upcoming Michael B. Jordan vehicle Fourth Wing could potentially become the show to outdo Game of Thrones, but that hotly anticipated series has another issue of its own. The first major romantasy adaptation, Fourth Wing will decide whether this massive publishing trend can translate to small screen success.

A portmanteau of romance and fantasy, romantasy is a subgenre that mixes romance of varying spice levels with settings, tropes and characters usually found in the world of fantasy novels. Where Terry Pratchett’s books mix fantasy and comedy, romantasy novels mix fantasy and romance, and this blend has become big business in the book world. A Court of Thrones and Roses alone has managed to sell over 13 million copies since the novel series began.

However, as the long wait for George R.R. Martin’s A Song of Ice & Fire to reach the screen proves, fantasy trends in publishing aren't always immediately seized on by movies and TV. Fantasy is expensive and, while Game of Thrones and its spinoffs have proven that there is a mainstream market for mature, R-rated fantasy shows, their tone could not further from the romantic atmosphere of books like Fourth Wing.

Written by Rebecca Yarros, 2023’s Fourth Wing is the first in the Empyrean Pentalogy, followed by Iron Flame and Onyx Storm. Prime Video's Fourth Wing show will adapt the book’s immersive fantasy story, which follows the 20-year-old Violet Sorrengail as she is forced by her strict army general mother to become a dragon rider at the Basgiath War College, despite a challenging physical condition that makes her prone to injury.

While this set up might remind readers of How To Train Your Dragon, the reality of the books is much more adults-only in nature. The Empyrean Pentalogy is unapologetically adult in its content, with a lot of explicit sex scenes surprisingly early on in its enemies to lovers story. Describing the plot any further would run the risk of giving away who these scenes are between, but it is no spoiler to say the story’s romance elements owe as much tonally similar to Bridgerton than Game of Thrones.

Although Game of Thrones was extremely explicit, the fantasy show’s sex scenes were as apt to be disturbing as they were to be romantic. In contrast, the Fourth Wing books are mainstream romance novels in terms of their sexual content, and this means that the show’s big-budget live-action adaptation will have a markedly different tone from earlier major fantasy shows.

From the PG-rated Percy Jackson & The Olympians to Prime Video’s relatively family-friendly The Rings of Power, all the way to hard-R-rated, adult fantasy shows like House of the Dragon, A Knight of the Seven Kingdoms, and The Witcher, a heavy focus on romance and frequent sex scenes simply aren’t commonplace in the genre on the small screen. As such, some fantasy fans will no doubt be put off by Fourth Wing’s style.

Inflation Just Hit 3.7%. History Says to Own Companies That Can Do This 1 Thing.

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Businesses that possess this valuable characteristic can better navigate the current environment.

Investors won't get a fresh inflation update for another week. However, the trend has been strikingly clear.

The Consumer Price Index rose 3.7% during the month of September. This closely watched metric is still well ahead of the Federal Reserve's long-run 2% target, a level last seen in early 2021. And it marked an acceleration from August.

It's impossible to know when inflation will cool down. Investors are forced to deal with this reality. History says in this type of environment, it's a smart move to own companies that can do this one thing consistently.

If businesses are able to occasionally ask customers to pay more, it requires selling products and services that are truly differentiated. Only high-quality companies fall into this category. They should be on your watch list.

Pricing power is a feature that's developed over long periods of time. It doesn't appear out of thin air. Companies that have it possess some form of economic moat, such as a beloved brand that resonates strongly with customers relative to rival offerings. This usually shows up in the financials through high gross margins and returns on invested capital.

Just as consumers face inflation in their daily lives, companies can feel the same in their cost structures. If they have proven pricing power, it's much easier for them to handle inflationary pressures. They can pass higher costs on to their customers, thereby protecting their profitability.

This provides a unique advantage. Weaker businesses aren't so fortunate. If they raise prices, there's a greater likelihood that they'll lose customers who find their products and services easily substituted. Elite companies don't have this problem, as their earnings power can hold up in inflationary times. And this is where investors need to look.

Apple (AAPL -1.53%) arguably has the world's most valuable brand. It's the perfect example of a high-quality business with pricing power. Its premium hardware devices register robust demand. Within its products segment, the company posted a 39.3% gross margin in fiscal 2026 (ended Sept. 26).

Customer loyalty comes not only from how well-designed and easy to use the products and services are, but from the proprietary ecosystem they combine to form. Once someone owns an iPhone, an Apple Watch, and an iCloud subscription, for example, it's extremely difficult to switch to a rival.

Apple has historically been able to raise prices on new devices without pushing buyers away. When inflation picks up component and labor costs, the company has room to pass them along.

Unlike mass-market carmakers, Ferrari (RACE -2.32%) deliberately limits its supply to maintain the status and exclusivity of its brand name. During the three months that ended Sept. 30, it shipped just 3,368 vehicles. And there are long wait lists for the newest models.

That scarcity is what supports pricing power. Wealthy consumers view these as collectibles. And they aren't deterred by price increases, which might actually drive even more demand. For select special-edition models that cost seven-figure sums, Ferrari collects sizable deposits well before delivery.

The company's profitability speaks for itself. Ferrari just reported a Q3 operating margin of 29.7%, which is typical of a luxury brand. If needed, the company has no problem charging customers more.

In the world of financial services, American Express (AXP -2.29%) stands out. Its pricing power shows up in what it charges its cardholders. In the past 10 years, the average fee per card has risen by 177.6%.

In September 2025, American Express increased the annual fee of its flagship Platinum Card from $695 to $895, a notable bump. "One year after the U.S. Consumer Platinum refresh, spend retention remains at 99% for the portfolio," chief financial officer Christophe Le Caillec said on the Q3 2026 earnings call.

People pay more because they clearly value the perks, rewards, and benefits American Express offers. And there's a certain level of prestige with having these cards in your wallet. Additionally, the 190 million merchant locations are comfortable paying higher processing fees because they get access to affluent customers.

Apple, Ferrari, and American Express, all stocks that have crushed the market in the past decade, are outstanding businesses. Investors shouldn't rush to buy their shares, though, as it's critical to assess their valuations before putting money to work.

American Express is an advertising partner of Motley Fool Money. Neil Patel has no position in any of the stocks mentioned. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends American Express, Apple, and Ferrari. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

Opinion: Wow, they are here, the new Roberto Firmino and Mo Salah have arrived

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

A new duo are on the scene, replicating Mo Salah and Roberto Firmino.

Things were different when you tuned into every Liverpool match and these two would be lining up for the Reds.

Firmino smiling pearly whites glaring up at the screen. His twinkle touches and mesmerising movements. Combine that with his silky skills and tireless running.

The Brazilian made things look simple. But he was the glue in the spine of Jurgen Klopp's team. Without Firmino, there is simply no Liverpool success.

Mo Salah is the same. He was different. Explosive and clinical. Unplayable in his prime. Salah was very much the star of the team. The Egyptian King, who we all adored and loved.

He broke records and became one of the most important faces of the club's history in the modern era.

Liverpool's attack miss Firmino and SalahThe most free-flowing attacking football the Reds played was when both of them were still at the club. Firmino departed in 2023, and then Salah has followed this summer. Liverpool have some exciting attackers. Expensive attackers, too. They have all proven themselves at the highest level. But the reality is that what we have seen (so far) is nowhere near at the same level as Salah and Firmino. Sure, Alexander Isak is scoring a lot of goals. Bradley Barcola has moments of flair, and Rio Ngumoha is an extremely tricky winger. Meanwhile, Victor Munoz is hard to contain when he's playing his best football. However, the chemistry between them just isn't there yet. Over time, it might be. Andoni Iraola is undoubtedly working on that in the training ground.

If things don't work out, you can always replace players and bring new ones in. A team is never complete. Not in modern football. You constantly have to evolve and give new arsenal to your managers. Two players who Liverpool might be looking at are Nico Paz and Anastasios Douvikas. The former has 14 goal contributions in 13 matches this season. Meanwhile, the latter has 12 in 14 matches. Both are playing for Como at the moment, and recently, they achieved a feat that only Salah and Firmino have done together in the past. According to WhoScored, Douvikas and Paz are the first teammates to both achieve a 10.0 match rating in the same UEFA Champions League fixture since April 2018 when Salah and Firmino achieved that feat against AS Roma. Perhaps, it's time to bring both of them to Anfield.

Transfer news LIVE: Arsenal eye triple deal; huge Man Utd manager twist; Chelsea, Liverpool, Tottenham latest

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The January window is fast approaching as Arsenal, Chelsea, Manchester United, Liverpool and Tottenham all pursue some huge new mid-season signings to help reach their goals. Arsenal are said to be targeting a move for Antonio Nusa as they also eye Eli Junior Kroupi and Kenan Yildiz, with the likes of Alex Baena also believed to be in Mikel Arteta’s sights along with Julian Alvarez.

Didier Deschamps has reportedly turned down the chance to manage Manchester United, where Sunday’s game against Aston Villa will be crucial to determining the future of under-fire boss Michael Carrick. Tottenham are understood to still retain matching rights for Harry Kane after the England captain signed a record-breaking new Bayern Munich contract that is believed to make him the highest-paid player in Europe in terms of base salary.

Chelsea are confident of signing Alex Scott when the market reopens in the New Year, while Liverpool are keen on a swoop for Mexico wonderkid Gilberto Mora and Lamine Camara as Trent Alexander-Arnold continues to be linked with a sensational switch back to Anfield. Follow all the latest transfer news, rumours and gossip live below!

Arsenal are tracking Antonio Nusa ahead of the January transfer window, reports claim.

The Gunners are known to be keen on attacking reinforcements when the market reopens for business in the New Year and have reportedly had their eyes on Bournemouth forward Eli Junior Kroupi and Juventus winger Kenan Yildiz.

However, The Sun say that Arsenal are also keeping close tabs on former Club Brugge star Nusa, who continues to impress for both RB Leipzig and Norway.

Could a lucrative triple deal yet be in the offing at the Emirates Stadium after a somewhat disappointing summer?

Calciomercato claims that Juventus are considering a January move for Manchester United striker Benjamin Sesko despite his injury.

The Italian club have a €100million transfer budget following a capital injection and are assessing several attacking targets.

Folarin Balogun is also under consideration as Juventus explore options to strengthen their forward line.

Reports in Spain say that Barcelona have identified Arsenal defender Piero Hincapie as a potential signing for the January transfer window.

Sporting director Deco has made initial contact with the 24-year-old's representatives to explore a possible move.

Hincapie has struggled for regular minutes, but Mikel Arteta is reluctant to let players leave Arsenal in January.

Teamtalk claims that Manchester United have intensified their interest in Como defender Jacobo Ramon ahead of the 2027 transfer windows.

The 21-year-old is valued at around €50million, with Chelsea also keen and Arsenal, Liverpool and Fulham monitoring him.

Real Madrid hold a 50% sell-on clause and buy-back options, with a potential return to Spain under consideration.

Fichjaes claims that Barcelona have identified Manchester City left-back Rayan Ait-Nouri as a potential January signing.

The 25-year-old has made just three appearances this season, with City valuing him at around €30million.

Barca could pursue a deal if Alejandro Balde leaves, while Manchester United are also interested.

Unai Emery will not be going to Manchester United.

The former Arsenal and PSG boss is said to be a “dream target” for Old Trafford chiefs in the event that they sack Michael Carrick.

However, The Telegraph report that Emery is in advanced talks over signing a new long-term contract at Villa Park.

Villa said yesterday that Emery had been involved in an “important working meeting to plan the club's future and lay the foundations for its long-term sporting and institutional project”.

That meeting - in which they discussed committing to joint development beyond 2029 under Emery’s leadership - also involved chairman Nassef Sawiris, president of football operations Roberto Olabe and director of football operations Damian Vidagany.

Cristiano Ronaldo has retired from international duty with Portugal.

The country’s greatest-ever player announced his decision via a video to camera on his social media channels.

Ronaldo netted 146 goals in 234 games for Portugal, becoming the all-time leading scorer in the history of men’s international football.

The striker was suspended by the Portuguese FA last month after he stormed out of the Nations League camp in October, having fallen out with manager Jorge Jesus.

Chelsea are interested in signing Everton star Harrison Armstrong, reports claim.

The England Under-21 international has established a reputation as one of the Premier League’s most impressive young midfielders under David Moyes.

TEAMtalk cite reports claiming that Chelsea are compiling weekly scouting reports on the 19-year-old, who continues to go from strength to strength at the Hill Dickinson Stadium.

Chelsea are widely expected to swoop for another top young midfielder in January in Bournemouth’s Alex Scott, 23.

Harry Kane’s bumper new Bayern Munich contract has now been confirmed.

The Ballon d’Or winner has been in talks with the reigning Bundesliga champions for months now regarding a lucrative extension to his previous deal that was due to expire next summer.

Kane is now tied to Bayern until 2029 on a record-breaking contract that is said to make him the highest-paid player in Europe by salary.

The England captain will be 36 by the time it expires, meaning he may well now never return to the Premier League and looks destined to stay second on the list of all-time top scorers behind Alan Shearer.

Manchester United looks set to make a sensational loan swoop for Ivan Toney in January, reports claim.

The struggling Red Devils are desperate for more firepower under Michael Carrick with first-choice striker Benjamin Sesko out until January with his lingering shin injury.

According to Football Insider, United want England star Toney, who now plays his football in Saudi Arabia with Al-Ahli.

They are more likely to make a loan move for the former Brentford frontman than a permanent offer, however.

Singapore Airlines Unveils Posh New First Class Suites With Double Beds For 777-9 And A350ULR

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Singapore Airlines has unveiled its next-generation first class seat, with sliding doors, companion dining, and center seats that combine into a double bed measuring five feet across. The new product will appear on the Airbus A350-900ULR and Boeing 777-9, bringing first class to the airline’s ultra-long-range A350 fleet for the first time.

Singapore Airlines’ new first class specifications reveal a small cabin with substantial personal space. The A350-900ULR will have four seats, while the Boeing 777-9 will have six. Both use a 1-2-1 arrangement with direct aisle access and a curved cabin layout.

PriestmanGoode designed the product, with Collins Aerospace manufacturing the seats. The interiors feature Poltrona Frau leather and padded walls incorporating Singapore Airlines’ batik motif.

The centerpiece is the pair of center seats. A fully retractable divider allows passengers to share the space, with the seats moving together to create either a two-person sofa or a double bed. That bed measures 80 inches long and 60 inches wide. Singapore Airlines describes it as the largest double bed in commercial aviation.

Solo passengers receive an 80-inch-long, 30-inch-wide bed. The seat itself measures 23 inches across with the armrests raised, expanding to 30 inches when they are lowered.

Every seat also supports face-to-face dining with a companion, a feature I appreciate in first class: traveling together should include the option to sit across from one another for dinner, even when you prefer separate window seats.

The seat includes electronically adjustable side wings, upper and lower lumbar support, adjustable armrests, a leg rest, and an ottoman. Integrated myKrisWorld controls can save preferred seat positions and lighting settings for future flights.

For sleeping, the crew will prepare the bed with cotton linens, a duvet, a choice of pillows, and a new mattress topper from German bedding company Paradies. Singapore Airlines says the topper uses breathable spacer fabric to improve cushioning and temperature regulation.

Dedicated storage includes a personal wardrobe, a leather-lined vanity drawer, and space for cabin baggage beneath the ottoman.

Both aircraft receive a 32-inch 4K entertainment screen, but the A350ULR version is OLED. The 777-9 specification lists a conventional 4K display.

Passengers will be offered Bang & Olufsen wireless headphones or can pair Bluetooth headphones with the system. A separate touch panel displays flight information, connectivity status, and lavatory availability without interrupting the main screen.

The new cabins are scheduled to begin entering service in the second half of 2027. Unlike with ANA, the unveiling does not signal an imminent onboard debut. First class is planned for the A350-900ULR and 777-9. The standard long-haul A350-900 will receive the new business class, but no first class cabin.

Singapore Airlines’ new first class looks lovely, particularly for couples who can use the center seats as a shared living and sleeping space. Companion dining at every seat and substantial bedding are welcome details too.

Swansea City loanee is ready to break into the side after slow start: Opinion

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Swansea City have made a great start to this season and are one of the early title contenders. However, some defensive injury concerns have seen them fall away in the last four, losing three in that process.

Centre-back duo Ben Cabango and Stephen Welsh are both currently nursing knocks. Just as Cabango looks to be over those woes, Welsh went off with a knee injury in the defeat to Portsmouth, which is a blow but not as bad as first believed.

The Swans injury problems have created an opportunity for Jenson Seelt, and the early signs suggest the Dutchman has the attributes to make the most of it. The 23-year-old arrived on loan from Sunderland in August, providing Vitor Matos with some much-needed depth.

However, there is potential for the move to become much more significant for all parties involved.

Seelt is a physically imposing centre-back who offers aerial strength, aggression and reasonable composure in possession. Those qualities are particularly valuable in Matos’ system, which demands defenders who can contribute to the build-up while remaining comfortable defending aggressively and winning their individual battles.

The Swansea boss has already highlighted Seelt’s physicality, aerial dominance, composure and reading of the game. His early numbers suggest those qualities are translating onto the pitch.

According to FotMob, he ranks in the 79th percentile for aerial duels won and the 92nd percentile for defensive contributions among centre-backs. Across six Championship appearances and 230 minutes, Seelt has won 12 aerial duels at a success rate of 63.2 per cent.

He has also recorded 22 clearances, seven interceptions, 11 recoveries and 34 defensive contributions. His 82.4 per cent passing accuracy, alongside 16 line-breaking passes, also suggests he has the ability to contribute when Swansea are building from the back.

There is still plenty of work to do, however. Seelt has only started twice and needs a sustained run of games before any definitive conclusions can be drawn. Nevertheless, his parent club Sunderland clearly believe there is considerable potential to unlock.

The Black Cats handed Seelt a new contract until 2030 before allowing him to join Swansea, with director of football Florent Ghisolfi making clear that the defender remains part of their long-term plans.

If he can establish himself under Matos, improve his consistency and become a dependable presence in a side competing towards the top end of the division, he could return to Wearside in a much stronger position.

Swansea have acquired a defender whose physical profile suits their immediate needs, Seelt has the platform to prove himself, and Sunderland retain control of a promising young player. This loan could prove to be an extremely smart piece of business for all three parties.

Martine Rose is the New Creative Director of Balenciaga

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Martine Rose is getting her flowers. The London-based designer, known for her beloved eponymous menswear label, is the new creative director of Balenciaga, the company announced on Friday.

She was long tipped to take the helm at the French maison, having helped shape the brand’s Demna era as a menswear consultant from 2015 to 2018, just as he conceived its signature oversized clothes. Now, she is returning with creative control over the whole house, succeeding outgoing creative director Pierpaolo Piccioli, who departed on Thursday after just three seasons in the role. Rose will take on the role, based in Paris, effective November 16. Rose will be the first female creative director at Balenciaga in its 109-year history.

“Balenciaga has always been a house of radical creativity, challenging conventions and redefining the boundaries of fashion. Martine Rose embodies this spirit with a fresh, fearless and deeply contemporary vision,” Kering CEO Luca de Meo said in a statement. “Her connection to the new generation and her instinctive understanding of culture bring a new dimension to the house. Her appointment reflects our ambition to keep Balenciaga at the forefront of innovation, creativity and culture.”

“Martine Rose is an extraordinary creative talent with a bold and highly distinctive vision,” Balenciaga president and CEO Gianfranco Gianangeli said. “Her ability to challenge conventions, reinvent silhouettes and bring an unexpected perspective to fashion resonates deeply with the spirit of innovation that defines Balenciaga. We are delighted to welcome her as creative director and look forward to this exciting new chapter for the house.”

Kendrick Lamar wearing a custom Martine Rose jacket at his Super Bowl performance in 2025.

Rose carries a lot of industry goodwill. Since rumors began swirling on social media about her appointment, scores of editors, stylists, influencers and celebrities rejoiced online. Her offbeat menswear, inspired by British subcultures from football casuals to rave kids, skinheads to reggae heads, has won the designer many fans since she launched her eponymous label in 2007. Not least Kendrick Lamar, who posted about Rose after she made looks for his Big Steppers tour in 2022: “Bucket list to work with Martine Rose…” he posted to his then-12 million Instagram followers, alongside a photo of Rose peace-signing into the camera. Other celebrity fans include Rihanna, A$AP Rocky and Timothée Chalamet, all of whom have been spotted wearing her oversized, sports-infused fits in recent years.

The Balenciaga appointment has come at a good time for the designer. She has faced major challenges at her eponymous label in recent years, since selling a 60% stake in the business to incubator Tomorrow Ltd in 2023. Rose was in a dispute with Tomorrow Ltd over the handling of her brand, which became apparent when she announced she would not produce her FW26 collection in March. Tomorrow released a counter statement, and ultimately, as major shareholders of the brand, they were able to produce the collection, despite Rose’s announcement.

Shortly after, in March of this year, Tomorrow sold Rose’s brand to Italian company The Level Group (Paris label Coperni was also bought by The Level Group, and the founders announced this week that they will shutter the brand). Rose has not staged a fashion show since SS26 last June.

Martine Rose’s last fashion show for SS26.

Another designer in the Tomorrow/The Level Group’s stable, Charles Jeffrey, posted a video online on October 26, explaining how thrilled he would be if the Martine x Balenciaga rumors were true. “She would be so incredible in the role because she speaks to a community that doesn’t see themselves in the [luxury] space,” he says, “and she does it with such warmth.”

In his ReconKering turnaround plan, Kering CEO Luca de Meo explained that he sees Balenciaga as a house for Gen Z. “Balenciaga is one of the brands that better understands the vibes of the new generation,” he said during the group’s Capital Markets Day in April. Rose also understands those vibes. From her signature entry-level T-shirts, spotted frequently in the Vogue and GQ offices, to her longstanding tie-in with Nike, including the popular Shox mules, Rose knows how to make a hit item that chimes with Gen Z. As the luxury industry grapples with the loss of the aspirational shopper, her high-low approach could be a boon to Balenciaga, as it tries to recapture its young Demna-era fans.

The Nike Shox MR4 x Martine Rose Safety Orange sneakers, worn during Paris Fashion Week Men’s.

Balenciaga’s menswear business was a big opportunity under Demna, so Rose — best known for her men’s — is well placed to continue that trajectory. De Meo also plans to double Balenciaga’s women’s ready-to-wear by 2030, which will be a task for Rose, as she gets used to the new development capabilities of a major house. She doesn’t have the couture experience of a big-name designer like Piccioli. That said, many women already wear her men’s looks, and she has amped up her womenswear output in recent years, creating co-ed collections since 2023. Accessories will be another focus: de Meo added that leather goods continue to become Balenciaga’s most powerful growth engine (led by its Rodeo and City bags); the goal is to further accelerate the leather goods business by 2030.

"It is a true privilege to be appointed creative director of Balenciaga, a house with an extraordinary heritage and a fearless creative spirit,” Rose said. “Balenciaga has been shaped by remarkable creative talents before me. I am committed to honoring this legacy and look forward to creating the next chapter in this rich and beautiful story.”

Rose’s appointment is part of a wider shake-up at Kering, including Anthony Vaccarello’s exit from Saint Laurent, and Seán McGirr’s departure from Alexander McQueen. Their successors are yet to be announced, but many of the names swirling around both roles are women, meaning that by the end of the year, Kering could be a conglomerate with majority-women creative directors.

Arsenal transfer news: ‘You couldn’t blame’ Saliba for Real Madrid move, warns former Gunner

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

By Axel Clody | 06 Nov 2026 06:59

William Gallas has spoken about Real Madrid’s interest in William Saliba in recent months. The former defender does not see the French international turning down the Spanish giants.

During the last summer transfer window, Arsenal rejected a £144.5m (€170m) swap deal for William Saliba with Real Madrid. Real Madrid were prepared to send Rodrygo to London to land the French international. Arsenal immediately rejected the proposal and made the former Saint-Etienne player untouchable.

In September 2025, the centre-back extended his contract with the English club until June 2030. A sign of long-term commitment? According to William Gallas, the 25-year-old will find it hard to turn down a fresh approach from the Spanish giants.

‘Are Arsenal at a high enough level now that William Saliba would turn down Real Madrid if they came in for him?’ the former Arsenal defender (2006-2010) wondered in an interview with SeatPick, before answering his own question: ‘I know a lot of people will be mad about what I’m going to say right now, but if we are talking about the history of both clubs, Real Madrid and Arsenal, then Real Madrid is Real Madrid. They are on a different level.’

So, although Arsenal are once again establishing themselves as a powerhouse in England and Europe, Gallas believes Real Madrid, who have not won a trophy for a year and a half, remain a more attractive destination, even a dream for players.

‘Madrid is Madrid, and I’m telling you, any player who has the opportunity to go to Madrid, they will try to go there, and you can’t blame them. You couldn’t blame a player like Saliba if that is his decision,’ added the 49-year-old. Could a transfer take place?

Real Madrid may soon need to strengthen the heart of their defence. Antonio Rudiger is under contract until June 2027, while Eder Militao’s deal expires in June 2028 and Raul Asencio does not appear to feature in Jose Mourinho’s plans.

Real Madrid could therefore try their luck again with William Saliba in 2027.

If the 25-year-old Frenchman, who is valued at £85m (€100m) on the market, opens the door to an exit, Arsenal will have no interest in doing the Spanish giants any favours and will surely want to set a new record for the transfer of a defender.

That record is held by Josko Gvardiol, who joined Manchester City for £76.5m (€90m). It is a figure that would make everyone think twice, even Real Madrid.

XRP Ledger's Biggest Lending Upgrade Just Got Closer

📅 2026-11-06  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The XRP Ledger (XRPL) is on the verge of a significant decentralized finance (DeFi) upgrade that could potentially open up new lending possibilities and liquidity for the XRP Ledger network.

According to Vet (@Vet_X0), a notable XRPL validator and community member, the much-anticipated Lending Protocol amendment has now gained two more Yes votes from validators, moving one step closer to activation.

The Lending Protocol amendment now boasts 21 Yes votes from a total of 35 trusted validators (60%). The amendment needs eight more Yes votes to reach the minimum threshold of 29 validators.

Reaching this threshold doesn’t mean that the upgrade will instantly go live. Instead, reaching the supermajority threshold will initiate the mandatory two-week activation period, which requires a minimum of 80% of validators to support the amendment.

The upgrade, known as XLS-66, is geared towards adding a native lending feature to the XRP Ledger.

In contrast to most crypto lending services, where borrowers are typically required to provide more collateral than the loan amount, XLS-66 is specially crafted to enable fixed-term and non-collateralized lending.

This will enable qualified borrowers to secure loans without the need to hold cryptocurrency collateral that exceeds the loan amount.

According to the official XLS-66 document, the Lending Protocol will enable the pooling of funds via so-called Single Asset Vaults, which can then be loaned to borrowers under set terms.

The proposed lending system involves three key actors: depositors, loan brokers and borrowers.

While depositors are in charge of depositing crypto or supported assets into a vault, loan brokers are tasked with brokering lending deals as well as evaluating borrowers.

For their part, borrowers would receive the loans and pay them back based on the set schedule.

The upgrade could potentially empower financial institutions and other entities to put capital to use on the XRP Ledger, as opposed to just holding or moving around assets.

It may also open up new prospects for earning interest on deposited assets in lending vaults. The amount of interest, however, would largely depend on the actual lending activity and risks.

According to the latest validator data, this amendment has reached 23 out of 35 votes. It still requires six more votes to hit the threshold.

Furthermore, the LendingProtocolV1_1 amendment, which offers upgrades including closed-ended vaults, updated interest accounting, etc., also currently has 21 Yes votes.

All three amendments are currently being considered, and none has yet reached supermajority.

Personal Investments • Re: How to incorporate an inheritance into retirement plan??

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

We have an extremely high likelihood of inheriting an amount approximately equal to our retirement savings and I’m looking for some creative ways to account for this in our retirement planning.

Please do not reply with any version of “I do not count on inheriting any money in my planning” or “Do count on inheritance because you don’t know what will happen”. If you respond this way, it will be evident that you did not read my post and just jumped to replying.

This is the situation.
Me: 60
Wife: 55

Our retirement will be funded by a non-cola pension, social security, and our retirement savings (401k and IRA). We also have money outside of the ‘retirement’ accounts for emergency/family obligations (weddings)/upcoming necessary house projects/etc.

Exact date of retirement is still TBD but most likely for me between 62 and 65. For my wife really TBD as she owns her own business so retirement may be some sort of soft retirement as she really enjoys working her business.

Our baseline retirement has always been 65/65 and our Fidelity Monte Carlo score is usually between 95 and 100. Even without the inheritance we are realizing that earlier than 65 is strongly likely and are currently evaluating it without any inheritance.

So, between my mother and my wife’s mother we stand to inherit an amount similar to our retirement balance. My mother is 93 and hers is 86. Each are not touching the principal of their savings, both have good LTC insurance, both have very good financial advisors along with a trustworthy family member watching their finances. Their portfolios could be best described as Boglehead like. And both are in relatively good physical and mental health, though my mother is obviously aging more quickly over that last few years.

The obvious Boglehead approach would be not to account for this blessed windfall in our retirement projection. BUT this creates another type of loss and that is the missed opportunities of life, not only for myself and my wife but also for our children and spouses. Something my mother reminds me of because my father was too much of a Boglehead and past before doing all the things he wanted to. It’s one regret my mother has and one I am determined to minimize.

So how would you work into your retirement plan a rather secure potential windfall as I’ve described. One way I’m considering is to cut the amount in half and place it out at the age of 101 and spread it over numerous years. The odds of our mothers reaching 101 are about 10%. Since we are well funded to begin with an addition year or two would not impact us that much.

As for assuming one half of the inheritance, I feel this is very conservative as the portfolio is very Boglehead centric, well managed, and has very little risk of unexpected costs due to LTC insurance and neither owning a house. And if we did inherit it at a reduced amount, just like our retirement funds we would expect it to recover somewhat over the years.

Any thoughts or suggestions with respect to modeling this eventual windfall?
BRP_Explorer hit the nail on the head above in their post.

Do you know if both inheritances will be in traditional IRA, Roth IRA, taxable account, any annuities, etc?

The reason we ask is your answers will address some of your quest you ask of us.

If traditional IRA, then you will have a 10 year window to withdraw it, and it will be taxed as ordinary income. Plenty of ways to mitigate some of that based on how you are contributing to your own workplace plans. It also leads to options of utilizing your "window" before claiming SS and your own RMDs begin at age 75 to do calculated Roth conversions. If taxable, you get the step up in basis, but need to decide how the taxable investments fit with your current AA.

In short, we don't have enough information to give you much guidance at this point. Meanwhile, you just continue contributing to your own workplace plans to create your own DIY cola for your non-cola pension, stuff as much as you can into your Roth space (Roth IRAs as well as perhaps using your workplace Roth 401 if available) to create as much tax diversification you can to be able to handle the additional inheritance. Pension + SS + withdrawals from a sizable portfolio via traditional RMDs can create a tax torpedo (especially if one of you passes years before the other leaving the survivor with a larger tax bill). That is what our guidance would be to you without knowing how large your own portfolio is and what size and kind of investments you will be inheriting.

CyclingDuo

He didn’t mention kids but sometimes it may make sense to pass on the IRAs directly to adult children, in full or in part, if they will be in a lower tax bracket, and assuming they are responsible enough to handle it.

Chiefs linked to star wide receiver to help Patrick Mahomes originally appeared on The Sporting News. Add The Sporting News as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

The NFL's much-anticipated trade deadline is coming up on Tuesday, Nov. 10, which means it's down to the wire for teams to pick up the phone and trade with each other. After the trade deadline passes, player contracts won't be assigned or traded from one team to the next, marking the end of any trade activity for the season. The deadline is specifically 4 p.m. ET on Nov. 10.

In a feature released on Thursday by ESPN, Bill Barnwell rattles off a handful of trade ideas that he sees as fitting for teams that are real contenders to go deep in the playoffs.

The Kansas City Chiefs, of course, are one of those teams. This team is hot right now. Sure, they just dropped a game, but the Chiefs are still 5-2 for the season and making a run for the postseason. Barnwell sees this team helping quarterback Patrick Mahomes with a smart deal.

The Chiefs could use help on offense, and Mahomes could certainly benefit from another great wide receiver. Barnwell lays out a few options.

He really pinpoints star wideout Brian Thomas Jr., whom he calls a "dream addition for the Chiefs at wide receiver." However, he warns that the Jacksonville Jaguars likely won't trade him to "their rivals in the AFC."

But there are other top wideouts on the market. There's a second star Barnwell mentions.

"Would the 3-5 Cowboys consider trading George Pickens, who is a free agent after the season and unlikely to be back on a long-term deal?" he asks. "Pickens would probably be a short-term fix for Kansas City, but it doesn't have anyone who can win on the outside like him. He adds, "If the Chiefs play free agency right, they should be able to land a fourth- or fifth-round pick for Pickens as a compensatory selection next offseason."

Either way, the Chiefs could always use more help at wideout, and that would really help make Mahomes even more explosive.

Why Krispy Kreme Stock Crushed It Today

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Investors are savoring the glazed doughnut seller's improved profitability.

Shares of Krispy Kreme (DNUT +30.68%) surged more than 30% on Thursday after the doughnut maker boosted its full-year sales forecast.

Krispy Kreme's net revenue declined 12.4% year over year to $328.8 million in its fiscal third quarter, which ended on Sept. 27.

The decline reflects the doughnut shop operator's shift toward a less capital-intensive, franchise-based business model. Roughly a quarter of the chain's systemwide sales -- the total dollar value of all Krispy Kreme product sales -- came from franchise-operated stores in fiscal 2025. That figure rose to 42% in the third quarter.

Management is targeting 50% of systemwide sales generated by franchisees by fiscal 2027. Third-quarter systemwide sales increased 1% to $490.2 million.

Refranchising is a key part of Krispy Kreme's turnaround strategy. Selling company-operated stores to franchisees is helping Krispy Kreme pay down debt and improve its profit margins and cash flow.

The company's Q3 adjusted net income rose sevenfold to $9.9 million, or $0.06 per share.

Looking ahead, Krispy Kreme lifted its full-year net revenue target range to $1.35 billion to $1.4 billion, up from a prior forecast of $1.25 billion to $1.35 billion.

Systemwide sales are projected to rise 2% to 4% to $1.96 billion, driven by at least 100 franchised store openings. The company also expects to generate free cash flow of over $15 million.

"Our results demonstrate the success of the actions we are taking to drive sustainable, profitable growth," CEO Josh Charlesworth said.

Blue Origin says it is building a higher-performing rocket engine at 1/4 the cost

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

KENNEDY SPACE CENTER, Fla.—Blue Origin announced two significant upgrades to its New Glenn rocket on Thursday, reducing the cost of manufacturing the vehicle and expanding its capacity for payload delivery to geostationary orbit and the Moon.

With these upgrades, the company aims to eventually position New Glenn to launch more than 1,000 times a year.

“There has never been higher demand for launch, ever,” Dave Limp, chief executive officer of Blue Origin, said during the UP.Summit conference at the Shuttle Landing Facility in Florida.

Blue Origin has already begun test firing a new engine, called Northstar, that will eventually replace the BE-4 engine that powers the first stage of New Glenn. Separately, the company also said it was developing a hydrogen-oxygen third stage for the rocket, GS3, to give it an extra kick.

The Northstar engine is a simplified but more powerful version of the BE-4 engine, Limp said. It has half the number of parts, 30 percent less mass, and about 65 percent can be 3D-printed.

Blue Origin believes it can manufacture the engine at 25 percent of the cost of a BE-4 engine, and eventually reach the point where each engine can be assembled in less than a day. The company is deep into development and testing of Northstar, having already begun a test-firing campaign.

“To get access to space at a lower cost, we have to look at every part of the vehicle,” Limp said. “This is going to be a game-changer.”

The BE-4 engine powering New Glenn today has a thrust of 640,000 pounds, and Limp said Northstar will have a starting thrust of 715,000 pounds. Like the BE-4 engine, Northstar burns methane and liquid oxygen using an oxygen-rich staged combustion cycle and a single-shaft turbopump architecture.

Limp said the company will continue to manufacture the existing BE-4 engine for United Launch Alliance, which uses two of the engines to power its Vulcan rocket.

Blue Origin is developing a third stage for New Glenn called GS3 (creatively named Glenn Stage 3) to offer direct injection to geostationary orbit and Earth-escape trajectories. The third stage fits within an interstage, preserving the full fairing volume for customer payloads.

The stage can fly on both the smaller 7×2 variant (as in seven first stage engines and two second stage engines) of New Glenn as well as the larger 9×4 variant. According to Blue Origin, a GS3-equipped New Glenn 7×2 can carry 8 metric tons directly to geostationary orbit and 10 metric tons to translunar injection. Those figures increase to 14 tons and 20 tons, respectively, for the larger 9×4 variant.

As the rocket’s third stage, it needs to be light, Limp said. For this reason, the third stage is made mostly of composites, which are high-performing and lightweight. But there’s a problem with that.

The stage will be powered by the company’s BE-7 engine, already used by its lunar landers. This engine is optimized for in-space operations and uses liquid hydrogen and oxygen propellants. But hydrogen is the smallest atom and therefore leaky. With composites, thermal cycling and mechanical strain can cause cracking, exacerbating losses of molecular hydrogen.

Limp said the company was not sure a large composite tank, 5.5-meter diameter in this case, could be constructed that would contain hydrogen at high pressures.

The company recently had a breakthrough at Launch Site One in Texas, when it completed its first composite liquid hydrogen tanking test. During the test, the tank was pressurized to 100 percent of the maximum force expected during operation for 2.5 hours. It was even successfully tested at 150 percent of this maximum pressure for 10 minutes.

That gave the company confidence that a GS3 based on composite tanks was a concept that could go forward, and now the company is aiming for a debut in 2028 for a government customer.

“We have one customer signed up for cislunar space in 2028,” Limp said. “We are open for business.”

Ars Technica has been separating the signal from the noise for over 25 years. With our unique combination of technical savvy and wide-ranging interest in the technological arts and sciences, Ars is the trusted source in a sea of information. After all, you don’t need to know everything, only what’s important.

Gay Influencer Famed for Dancing in High Heels Faces Middle Age in Nine-Year Taiwan Creative Content Fest Documentary ‘My Silly Flirty Boys’

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

A gay influencer in Taiwan who became famous dancing in high heels, and the filmmaker who trained his camera on him for nine years, both stare down middle age in “My Silly Flirty Boys.”

The documentary from Taiwan-based Hong Kong filmmaker Frankie Sin is at the Taiwan Creative Content Fest (TCCF) in Taipei.

Sin followed Fufu from 2017 to 2026. Over that stretch, Fufu built a following with racy dance routines, performances in high heels and his online channel “FJ234,” where he talked openly about desire and helped bring drag queens and other LGBTQ+ performers to mainstream audiences. Not everyone approved. Critics who wanted gay men presented in a more respectable light branded him a “cancer of the gay community.”

Away from the spotlight, Fufu talks frankly about what fame has cost him and about the loneliness that sets in once the persona comes off. Now nearing 40, he is coming to terms with a face and body that are getting older.

“It began with my own anxiety over my queer identity and feeling out of place within the community’s mainstream standards, which led me to point my camera at a maverick who dared to challenge sexual taboos,” Sin says.

More than 10 years ago, Sin swapped one of Hong Kong’s conservative outlying islands for Taiwan, hoping its reputation for openness would give him a place to belong. What he found instead was a local gay scene that prized hyper-masculinity and a “model gay” image.

“This triggered a second wave of anxiety about my body and identity,” Sin says. “In 2016, I met Fufu, an influencer famous for dancing in high heels. His bold, confident display of desire stood in sharp contrast to my own insecurities and repression. He became a mirror for me.”

The shoot spans the years around Taiwan’s legalization of same-sex marriage and charts how queer entertainment grew and was then swallowed up by the mainstream market.

“As two gay men approaching 40 – one losing his spotlight, the other confronting his deepest insecurities – we both face a midlife crisis, learning how to make peace with aging, loneliness, and ourselves,” Sin says.

Fufu’s channel is shutting down this year, and with nearly all the key footage shot, the team is logging nine years of material ahead of a rough cut. Color grading, the sound mix, an original score and music clearances will follow, with picture lock targeted for the third quarter of 2027.

Stefano Centini and Chuti Chang serve as executive producers, with Henry Leong and Peter Yam producing. The producers say the film takes an honest look at the pecking order and shared anxiety inside Taiwan’s gay male community.

“By placing the personal changes of both men alongside the rise and fall of Taiwan’s queer entertainment industry, the film offers a rare, honest perspective with strong potential for cross-cultural resonance,” they say.

In Taipei, the team is looking for gap financing to finish post. It also wants to meet co-producers and sales agents from abroad, plus festival programmers, to help plan a worldwide release.

Sin is also developing two more feature documentaries about belonging and family. “An Island Boy” follows a young Hong Kong fisherman, while “Nomads of the Island” explores Sin’s own family history.

The Taiwan Creative Content Fest, organized by the Taiwan Creative Content Agency (TAICCA), runs Nov. 6–9 at the Taipei Nangang Exhibition Center.

Dolly Parton Estate Drops Lawsuit Against Late Star’s Nephew Over Alleged Threats

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Last week, lawyers for Bryan Seaver said they and the Parton estate had been "engaged in good faith discussions in an attempt to resolve this matter."

Dolly Parton’s estate has ended its lawsuit against Bryan Seaver, the late country star’s nephew and longtime head of security.

A Thursday (Nov. 5) court filing, obtained by Billboard, voluntarily dismisses the Parton estate’s claims against Seaver without prejudice, meaning they could be refiled again in the future. Lawyers for both sides did not immediately return inquiries as to whether the dismissal reflects a settlement, though Seaver’s attorneys wrote in a court filing last week that they had recently been “engaged in good faith discussions in an attempt to resolve this matter.”

She’s Alive LLC, the entity managing Parton’s posthumous affairs under the leadership of her longtime manager Danny Nozell, sued Seaver in September. The estate alleged Seaver had been trying to extort money since Parton’s death a month earlier by sending violent threats like “I’m a killer” and “all I do is warfare.”

Seaver denied this, saying Nozell had taken his text messages out of context and “turned a private, profane and ultimately friendly exchange between two longtime friends into a claim of terror.” Parton’s estate had been seeking an injunction to bar Seaver from interfering with the business (Seaver’s financial inheritance was never at issue in the legal case).

A hearing on the injunction request was delayed numerous times and had been set to finally begin this coming Tuesday (Nov. 10) in Nashville. That court proceeding is now canceled.

When Parton was alive, she was intentional about planning her estate to avoid the type of infighting that’s roiled grieving families of so many famous artists before her. But as the Seaver matter demonstrated, all the organization in the world still does not guarantee peace. “It only takes one to upset the apple cart,” estate attorney Scott Rahn told Billboard when the lawsuit was filed. But Rahn said the fact that Parton’s estate brought the matter to court so quickly was a sign that she had put the right people in place to take care of things.

“You cannot plan for every possible eventuality,” said Rahn at the time. “Your documents would fill libraries talking about every single thing. At the end of the day, what you need to do, which is I think what Dolly did, is pick a capable team for managing your affairs. And that’s what’s happening here.”

Janus Electric secures battery supply deal for its battery-swap semi truck

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Australian conversion specialists Janus Electric have been celebrating a growing slate of orders for their Class 8 electric truck conversions, which ditch diesel in favor of advanced battery-swap electric power – but exactly how they’d deliver all those trucks was still a question. Now, we have an answer: Electrovaya will supply the batteries.

The concept of dropping an electric power unit into a Class 8 semi makes perfect sense, as most of these trucks are built as gliders. That is, rolling body and chassis without an engine and transmission that’s ready to receive power from Cummins, Detroit, International, or Volvo Penta. That’s the role that Janus Electric sees itself occupying, and it seems to be working.

Janus’ reservation book crept past 70 units this past summer, helped along in part by California’s HVIP Program and the US EPA’s Diesel Emission Reduction Act (DERA) Grants. When the HVIP voucher is combined with the Port of Los Angeles’ “Plus” support, customers can received up to $166,000 per truck conversion. Factor in the massive fuel savings of electric and the classic good looks of square-nose Western Star truck body, and it’s easy to see why they’d be a popular choice.

As Jerry Seinfeld taught us however, it’s one thing to take a reservation – it’s another thing to deliver. To help make it happen, Janus has inked a battery supply deal with Electrovaya that will see a $26 million initial purchase orders for Electrovaya battery packs to demand for Janus’ products in the US and Canada, along with the battery company purchasing approximately $1.4 million in Janus stock with (what seems to be) actual money.

“Electrovaya is an important partner to Janus Electric, and we’re particularly pleased it has decided to invest directly in our business,” explains Ben Hutt, Managing Director and CEO of Janus Electric. “The investment is a strong vote of confidence in our technology and approach to electrifying heavy transport.”

The Janus Electric-repowered trucks carry a stout, 480 hp electric motor drawing power from a standard 310 kWh battery pack, with up to two battery packs (one on each side) of the truck. In practice, that gives the Janus trucks up to 370 miles of loaded range. After that, the batteries can be DC fast charged in under 90 minutes, or swapped for fresh ones in about four minutes without too much in the way of specialized equipment. That’s not just Janus’ killer app, it means that a battery supply deal like this one with a strategic partner who’s invested in the fledgling company’s success is such a huge deal.

“Reliable battery supply is critical to our growth,” adds Hutt, “and Electrovaya’s expanding North American manufacturing capabilities will be important as we scale. Both companies see considerable opportunity in this market, and we look forward to building on our partnership.”

For their part, Electrovoya executives seem to know their role here, and seem excited to be a part of what Janus is doing. “Class 8 trucking is one of the most exciting and demanding frontiers in electrification, and Janus has demonstrated real commercial traction with fleets in the US, Canada and Australia,” said Dr. Raj Das Gupta, CEO of Electrovaya. “Janus has developed a practical solution to one of the major challenges facing heavy transport, enabling diesel-powered trucks to be converted to electric without compromising their commercial operations.”

Electrovaya will be expanding its American manufacturing capacity with lithium-ion cell, module, and battery pack production at its new, potentially BABA-compliant facility in Jamestown, New York, which is expected to come online in 2027.

Amnesiac Surgeon Confronts His Organ-Trafficking Past in Taiwan Creative Content Fest Series ‘Hannibal Inside’

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

An amnesiac sashimi chef learns he was once the star surgeon of an organ-trafficking syndicate in “Hannibal Inside,” a crime thriller series from Studio76 adapted from “The Monster Community,” a novel the producers picked up from Kadokawa Taiwan.

The project is at the Taiwan Creative Content Fest (TCCF) in Taipei.

Two years after a car crash erased his memory, Owen works the knife at a fishing port in Keelung, Taiwan. The only person he remembers is his girlfriend Xi-xi. When he steps in to save a stranger’s life on the street, footage of the rescue spreads online and his old associates spot him. Owen, it turns out, was the chief surgeon of Undercurrent, a syndicate that sells organs to the global rich. He was second in command only to its shadowy leader, known as The Professor. Related Stories Disney Expected to Lay Off Thousands of Staffers in Biggest Round of Cuts This Year These Coffee Table Books Make the Perfect Gift This Holiday Season

Undercurrent assassin Jiang Xu sets out to force Owen’s memories back to the surface. Meanwhile Detective Lin, whose son needs a heart transplant, pegs Owen as the prime suspect in a string of murders in which the victims’ organs are removed. After Xi-xi is killed, Owen heads to his hometown of Songkhla, Thailand, to take revenge. There he crosses paths with local police captain Xu Zhi-rou, whose father he once saved. The road leads to a luxury cruise ship where billionaires bid on living people according to their organ matches.

Producer Dennis Yang says the team took the serial killer plot of the book and wove in real news reports of black-market organ trading, “a high-risk underworld that is rarely explored with real emotional depth in Asian series.”

For director Ray Jiang, crime and action shows are plentiful, but few of them dig into what makes people tick or treat life with respect.

“I want to ask: ‘Who defines the value of a life? Who decides who gets to live and who dies?’” Jiang says. “Society often judges people by whether they are good or bad, or how much they contribute – as if criminals deserve to die, while good people should live forever. But I believe every life is valuable because of what a person achieves on their own journey. No one has the right to judge someone else’s worth.”

The series is in pre-development. Full episode outlines are complete and the writing team is working on scripts.

“This project blends commercial entertainment with a thrilling pace, while exploring universal questions of justice and survival. It is an ambitious crime thriller with great potential for the global market,” Yang says.

Yang calls the project’s selection for TCCF this year a major milestone. Two earlier Studio76 projects, “The Brides Town” and “The Swamp Thing,” were both picked to pitch in person at Series Mania in Lille, France, and Yang says “Hannibal Inside” marks the company’s third time.

At TCCF, the team hopes to meet IP investors and streamers from around the world, as well as distributors in Europe and co-production partners in Asia. “Ultimately, through TCCF and its bridge to Series Mania, we hope to bring our story to a wider global audience and maximize the project’s international IP potential,” Yang says.

The Taiwan Creative Content Fest, organized by the Taiwan Creative Content Agency (TAICCA), runs Nov. 6–9 at the Taipei Nangang Exhibition Center.

Simkhai Pre-Fall 2027 Menswear

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

As a designer mainly based in Los Angeles, Jonathan Simkhai can’t help but be influenced by the California coast: It’s in his fashion DNA. But if you think that means board shorts and flip-flops, think again. His pre-fall menswear fuses a free-spirited West Coast sensibility with a little more refinement—alternating between structure and ease. “We started with our great wardrobe heroes, and really played with color and fit,” he said.

Simkhai has been steadily building his menswear business these past few seasons, and his new assortment continued its focus on easy sportswear. Classic red polo sweaters were given sailor collars, and paired with his sleek pleated “Elvis” trousers. His blue-and-white striped polos were updated in cashmere-cotton blends that were soft to the touch. More technical windbreakers, a trending piece, were offered in subtle black and white or punchy red—the perfect thing to wear if you own a sailboat, and want to spend a day at sea. (One can dream.)

If things started to feel too coastal, Simkhai roughed them up with a bit of edge. The standout outerwear was a black funnel-neck leather jacket, boxy and with a sheen finish. Simkhai also offered luxurious suede button-up shirts that are great for those not-too-hot, not-too-cold days. His take on a classic denim jacket, meanwhile, was cropped and given a more nipped waistline.

Dexterra Group Inc. (DXT:CA) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Dexterra Group Inc. (DXT:CA) Q3 2026 Earnings Call November 5, 2026 8:30 AM EST

Denise Achonu - Chief Financial OfficerMark Becker - President, CEO & Director

Kyle McPhee - ATB Cormark Capital Markets Inc., Research DivisionTrevor Reynolds - Acumen Capital Finance Partners Limited, Research DivisionPrashant Kamath - National Bank Financial, Inc., Research Division

We have an extremely high likelihood of inheriting an amount approximately equal to our retirement savings and I’m looking for some creative ways to account for this in our retirement planning.

Please do not reply with any version of “I do not count on inheriting any money in my planning” or “Do count on inheritance because you don’t know what will happen”. If you respond this way, it will be evident that you did not read my post and just jumped to replying.

This is the situation.
Me: 60
Wife: 55

Our retirement will be funded by a non-cola pension, social security, and our retirement savings (401k and IRA). We also have money outside of the ‘retirement’ accounts for emergency/family obligations (weddings)/upcoming necessary house projects/etc.

Exact date of retirement is still TBD but most likely for me between 62 and 65. For my wife really TBD as she owns her own business so retirement may be some sort of soft retirement as she really enjoys working her business.

Our baseline retirement has always been 65/65 and our Fidelity Monte Carlo score is usually between 95 and 100. Even without the inheritance we are realizing that earlier than 65 is strongly likely and are currently evaluating it without any inheritance.

So, between my mother and my wife’s mother we stand to inherit an amount similar to our retirement balance. My mother is 93 and hers is 86. Each are not touching the principal of their savings, both have good LTC insurance, both have very good financial advisors along with a trustworthy family member watching their finances. Their portfolios could be best described as Boglehead like. And both are in relatively good physical and mental health, though my mother is obviously aging more quickly over that last few years.

The obvious Boglehead approach would be not to account for this blessed windfall in our retirement projection. BUT this creates another type of loss and that is the missed opportunities of life, not only for myself and my wife but also for our children and spouses. Something my mother reminds me of because my father was too much of a Boglehead and past before doing all the things he wanted to. It’s one regret my mother has and one I am determined to minimize.

So how would you work into your retirement plan a rather secure potential windfall as I’ve described. One way I’m considering is to cut the amount in half and place it out at the age of 101 and spread it over numerous years. The odds of our mothers reaching 101 are about 10%. Since we are well funded to begin with an addition year or two would not impact us that much.

As for assuming one half of the inheritance, I feel this is very conservative as the portfolio is very Boglehead centric, well managed, and has very little risk of unexpected costs due to LTC insurance and neither owning a house. And if we did inherit it at a reduced amount, just like our retirement funds we would expect it to recover somewhat over the years.

Any thoughts? Other suggestions?

San Francisco 49ers fans should expect the team to scour the NFL trade deadline market this season in search of more help in pursuit of a Super Bowl run.

This is one of those trade rumors that will be forgotten about in due time, as there is effectively no chance the 49ers land Taylor. It's purely speculation, and it's the kind that makes zero sense.
